RD : différence de risque (Risk Difference) ; RR : risque relatif (Risk Ratio) ; OR : Odds Ratio

HINE-2  (Hammersmith  Infant  Neurological  Examination-section  2) :  échelle évaluant  les  étapes  du  développement  moteur  (tenue  de  tête ;  tenue  assise ;  préhension  volontaire ;  mobilité  des
membres inférieurs ; retournement, ramper, position debout et marche). Elle contient 8 items. Plus le score est élevé, plus les capacités fonctionnelles sont importantes.

CHOP INTEND (Children’s Hospital Of Philadelphia Infant Test Of Neuromuscular Disorders) : échelle qui évalue la capacité des enfants atteints de SMA à exécuter certains mouvements. Elle
contient 16 items, notés pour la plupart de 0 à 4, 0 correspondant à l’absence de réponse ou de capacité à effectuer le mouvement et 4 correspondant à la capacité d’effectuer totalement la tâche.
Le score total possible est de 64.

MFM (mesure de fonction motrice) : échelle mesurant les capacités fonctionnelles motrices chez une personne atteinte d’une maladie neuromusculaire. Elle comporte 32 items, cotés avec une
échelle de 4 points et répartis en 3 domaines : station debout et transferts, motricité axiale et proximale, motricité distale.
CHOP INTEND (Children’s Hospital Of Philadelphia Infant Test Of Neuromuscular Disorders) : échelle qui évalue la capacité des enfants atteints de SMA à exécuter certains mouvements. Elle
contient 16 items, notés pour la plupart de 0 à 4, 0 correspondant à l’absence de réponse ou de capacité à effectuer le mouvement et 4 correspondant à la capacité d’effectuer totalement la tâche.
Le score total possible est de 64.
HINE-2  (Hammersmith  Infant  Neurological  Examination-section  2) :  échelle évaluant  les  étapes  du  développement  moteur  (tenue  de  tête ;  tenue  assise ;  préhension  volontaire ;  mobilité  des
membres inférieurs ; retournement, ramper, position debout et marche). Elle contient 8 items. Plus le score est élevé, plus les capacités fonctionnelles sont importantes.
HFMSE (Hammersmith Functional Motor Scale Expanded) : échelle évaluant les capacités fonctionnelles motrices globales. Elle est composée de 33 items cotés de 0 à 2 (exemple : lever la tête
en station allongée sur le dos, descendre quatre marches d’escalier). Plus le score est élevé, plus les capacités fonctionnelles sont importantes.
RULM (Revised Upper Limb Module) : échelle évaluant les capacités fonctionnelles des membres supérieurs. Elle contient 20 items cotés de 0 à 2 (exemple : soulever un poids de 500 g en
antéflexion d’épaule, déchirer un morceau de papier). Plus le score est élevé, plus les capacités fonctionnelles sont importantes.

HAS • Dépistage néonatal de l’amyotrophie spinale • juin 2024

DMP : différence moyenne pondérée (weighted average difference, WMD).

MFM32 (mesure de fonction motrice) : échelle mesurant les capacités fonctionnelles motrices chez une personne atteinte d’une maladie neuromusculaire. Elle comporte 32 items, cotés avec une
échelle de 4 points et répartis en 3 domaines : station debout et transferts, motricité axiale et proximale, motricité distale.
HINE-2  (Hammersmith  Infant  Neurological  Examination-section  2) :  échelle évaluant  les  étapes  du  développement  moteur  (tenue  de  tête ;  tenue  assise ;  préhension  volontaire ;  mobilité  des
membres inférieurs ; retournement, ramper, position debout et marche). Elle contient 8 items. Plus le score est élevé, plus les capacités fonctionnelles sont importantes.
HFMSE (Hammersmith Functional Motor Scale Expanded) : échelle évaluant les capacités fonctionnelles motrices globales. Elle est composée de 33 items cotés de 0 à 2 (exemple : lever la tête
en station allongée sur le dos, descendre quatre marches d’escalier). Plus le score est élevé, plus les capacités fonctionnelles sont importantes.
RULM (Revised Upper Limb Module) : échelle évaluant les capacités fonctionnelles des membres supérieurs. Elle contient 20 items cotés de 0 à 2 (exemple : soulever un poids de 500 g en
antéflexion d’épaule, déchirer un morceau de papier). Plus le score est élevé, plus les capacités fonctionnelles sont importantes.

EXPLORATOIRES :

Survie

Survie sans assistance respiratoire : 21/25 (durée médiane de suivi :
26 mois)

En  parallèle,  des  données  ont  été  collectées  chez  23  frères  et/ou
sœurs,  correspondant  à  19  liens  fraternels  avec  des  enfants  inclus
dans l’étude NURTURE. Au total, 8/23 frères et sœurs non traités au
stade  présymptomatique  étaient  décédés  avec  six  enfants  décédés
avant  l’âge  de  18  mois,  un  à  l’âge  de  84  mois  et  un  à  un  âge  non
connu.

Conclusion de la CT : considérant que 14 enfants parmi les frères et
sœurs non inclus dans NURTURE avaient une SMA de type 1 (ayant
débuté avant l’âge de 6 mois) et que l’histoire naturelle de la maladie
implique une espérance de vie souvent limitée à 2 ans chez ces pa-
tients, sans acquisition de la station assise, il est notable de constater
dans  l’analyse  de  suivi  en  date  du  29  mars  2019  issue  de  l’étude
NURTURE que chez 25 patients avec 2 à 3 copies du gène SMN2 et
un  âge  médian  de  34,8  mois  (min  :  25,7  –  max  :  45,4)  :  i)  tous  les
patients  étaient  vivants ;  ii)  tous  ont  atteint  la  capacité  de  s’asseoir
sans support ; iii) à l’âge de 24 mois, 72 % (18/25) des enfants n’ont
eu aucun symptôme de SMA.

Il persiste néanmoins des incertitudes sur la quantité d’effet et la com-
parabilité principalement liées à l’absence d’analyse comparative ro-
buste versus la fratrie ou un appariement qui aurait permis de mieux
estimer la quantité d’effet ; et sur l’évolution de ces patients, avec une
progression qui apparaît plus favorable qu’en l’absence de traitement,
mais sans guérison de la maladie.

HAS • Dépistage néonatal de l’amyotrophie spinale • juin 2024

Questionnaires
remplis par les
aidants (proxy)

Étude rétrospective

Données de qualité
de vie descriptives

* GCS : Generic Core Scale ; HUI : Health Utilities Index ; NMM : neuromuscular model ; Qdv : qualité de vie ; VAS (Visual Analogue Scale) : échelle visuelle analogue.

PedsQL (Pediatric Quality of Life Inventory) : échelle évaluant la qualité de vie des enfants et comprenant quatre dimensions : la santé physique, le fonctionnement émotionnel, le
fonctionnement social et le fonctionnement scolaire. Les réponses vont de 0 à 5 points (0 = jamais un problème, 5 = toujours un problème). Le score est ensuite adapté sur 100 et
inversé de telle sorte qu’un score élevé implique une meilleure qualité de vie.

EQ-5D (EuroQol-5D) : échelle de qualité de vie comprenant cinq dimensions : la mobilité, l’autonomie, les activités courantes, la douleur/gêne et l’anxiété/la dépression, évaluées
comme suit : aucun problème, problèmes légers, problèmes modérés, problèmes graves et beaucoup de problèmes. Utilisation d’une échelle visuelle analogue (VAS) allant jusqu’à
100.

HUI (Health Utilities Index) : échelle traduisant l’état de santé d’un individu (auto-questionnaire) sur les quatre dernières semaines. Les valeurs des réponses aux questions sont
transcrites en un index unique allant de 0 à 1 où 0 représente la mort et 1 une santé parfaite.

KIDSCREEN-27 : échelle de qualité de vie générique utilisée chez l’enfant de 8 à 17 ans et comprenant 27 items répartis en cinq dimensions : bien-être physique, bien-être psycholo-
gique, autonomie et relations avec les parents, autres patients (pairs) et soutien social, environnement scolaire. Chaque élément a été noté sur une échelle de Likert en 5 points allant
de 1 « pas du tout » à 5 « beaucoup ». Le score total est calculé en additionnant toutes les réponses (de 0 à 100 au total). Plus le score est élevé, meilleurs sont la qualité de vie et le
soutien social.

HAS • Dépistage néonatal de l’amyotrophie spinale • juin 2024

165

KINDL : échelle de qualité de vie générique utilisée chez l’enfant de 5 à 7 ans et comprenant 24 items répartis en six dimensions de quatre items chacune : bien-être physique, bien-
être émotionnel, estime de soi, famille, amis, école. Chaque élément a été noté sur une échelle de Likert en 5 points allant de 1 « jamais » à 5 « tout le temps », ou de manière inver-
sée (1 « tout le temps » à 5 « jamais ») selon les items (de 0 à 100 au total). Plus le score est élevé, meilleur est l’état de santé.

PROMIS Fatigue SF : outil mesurant l’impact de la fatigue chez le patient. Questionnaire rempli dans l’étude par le parent et fondé sur la fatigabilité (déclin des performances mo-
trices) et la fatigue perçue par le patient (fatigue ou manque d’énergie). Un score élevé est associé à une fatigue plus importante.

Méthodologie

  Étude bicentrique

  À l’inclusion, score total moyen entre 52,68 et 59,19, pour tous les

types de SMA

  À 18 mois, score total moyen entre 49,64 et 60,99, pour tous les

types de SMA

  N = 15 (56 %) patients avaient un score total plus élevé à la fin du

suivi et n = 12 (44 %) avaient un score identique ou inférieur

Pour l’ensemble des types de SMA, la variation annuelle moyenne du
score total a été estimée entre - 2,03 et 4,11 [- 10,58 à 4,11]

Concordance entre les scores VAS des patients et des parents :

Coefficient de corrélation de Spearman entre les scores VAS des pa-
tients et des parents à l’inclusion : 0,55 (p = 0,003) et à 18 mois : 0,76
(p < 0,001)

Entre l’inclusion et la fin du suivi, 38 % des parents ont rapporté une
augmentation du score VAS et 63 % un score identique ou inférieur

Commentaires
méthodologique
s

  Impact des traitements sur la qualité de

Impact des traitements sur la qualité de vie

vie

4/15 études rapportent l’impact des traitements : pas de différence si-
gnificative sur la qualité de vie

PedsQL (Pediatric Quality  of Life Inventory) : échelle évaluant la qualité  de vie des  enfants et comprenant quatre  dimensions : la  santé physique, le fonctionnement émotionnel, le
fonctionnement social et le fonctionnement scolaire. Les réponses vont de 0 à 5 points (0 = jamais un problème, 5 = toujours un problème). Le score est ensuite adapté sur 100 et
inversé de telle sorte qu’un score élevé implique une meilleure qualité de vie.

La  qualité  de  vie  est  jugée  meilleure  dans  la  SMA  de  type  II  par
rapport à la SMA de type I (p = 0,007).

La dimension liée à la communication est améliorée selon les auto-
questionnaires des enfants dans la SMA de type II par rapport au
type I. Elle est également améliorée dans les questionnaires proxy
dans la SMA de type II par rapport au type I, dans la SMA de type
II par rapport au type I et dans la SMA de type III par rapport au
type I.

Impact de la prise en charge thérapeutique

HAS • Dépistage néonatal de l’amyotrophie spinale • juin 2024

174

Assistance respiratoire : qualité de vie plus élevée pour les enfants
sans ventilation à pression positive sur le score total PedsQL par
auto-évaluation (68,2 vs 55,9, p = 0,97) et selon les parents (61,8
vs 50,5, p = 0 ,029).

Sonde de gastrostomie : qualité de vie plus élevée pour les enfants
sans sonde sur le score total PedsQL par auto-évaluation (68,2 vs
48, p = 0,41) et selon les parents (61,3 vs 41,6, p = 0,001) ;

nusinersen : pas de différence significative entre les enfants traités
et ceux non traités.

## p_3540244 : 2024-07-10_newborn_screening_for_spinal_muscular_atrophy_advance_assessment_of_extension_of_screening_to_the_general_population_in_fran

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  1
RECOMMENDING PUBLIC HEALTH STRATEGIES
 REPORT
Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy
Advance assessment of extension of screening to the general population in France
Validated by the HAS Board on 27 June 2024

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  2
Summary
Spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) is a rare genetic disease caused by bi-allelic mutations in the SMN1 gene. The protein encoded by this gene is involved in the survival of motor neurons in the spinal cord. The disease is characterised by the development of severe and progressive muscle weakness, which can begin in the first six months of life.
Between 2017 and 2021, a number of therapies emerged designed to limit or prevent the development of the disease, but these need to be administered to infants in the presymptomatic phase in order to optimise their efficacy. Newborn screening (NBS) for SMA would make it possible to identify the infants concerned before the onset and irreversible development of symptoms, thereby enabling optimum management.
The Haute Autorité de santé (French National Authority for Health, HAS) therefore sought to determine the benefit of including SMA in the national NBS programme, based on the six major criteria retained in the HAS’ methodological guide for the assessment of new candidate diseases.
Taking into account data from a systematic review of the literature and the SMA France registry, the intermediate results of the DEPISMA pilot study being conducted in two regions in France and delib-erations with the working group, the main conclusions are as follows:
Seriousness of the disease
SMA constitutes a group of progressive diseases, characterised by irreversible neuromuscular degen-eration. The most severe form results in death before the age of 2 years. In the literature, its incidence is estimated to be between 1/5,000 and 1/15,000 in Europe, depending on the country. Worldwide, it varies from 1/5,000 to 1/28,000. In France, the DEPISMA pilot study has estimated the incidence to be approximately 1/12,000 births.
Knowledge of the natural history of the disease
The natural history of SMA is known: infants are generally asymptomatic at birth, with the first signs appearing at a few weeks of age. However, the expression and severity of the disease are variable over time, with a high level of individual variability. In addition, the negative correlation between the number of copies of the SMN2 gene and disease severity is not absolute. According to the literature, individuals with four copies of the SMN2 gene and no copies of the SMN1 gene generally present a more moderate form of SMA than those with fewer copies of the SMN2 gene, with some remaining mildly symptomatic or asymptomatic into advanced age (
1
). However, a number of studies have re-ported a significant variability in phenotype and disease course in individuals with four copies of the SMN2 gene, with some cases developing the disease at between 1.5 and 4 years of age (
2
).
Performance of the screening test
In the literature, multiplex qPCR is described as being fast, precise and cost-effective. The performance of this screening test is systematically reported as being excellent, with a sensitivity and specificity of ≥ 95% and ≥ 99.9%, respectively, depending on the studies.
The DEPISMA pilot study, being conducted in two regions in France, aims to assess the feasibility of NBS for SMA on a population-wide scale. The intermediate results after one year report that the screening technique is reliable, with a sensitivity and specificity of 100%.
Timely information

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  3
For it to be useful, it is essential that the screening test result be available before the onset of symp-toms. Therefore, the value of newborn screening for SMA depends on the speed with which the infant is treated, before the development of irreversible neuron damage.
The data in the literature relating to SMA type I, the earliest-onset and most serious form of the disease, reports that in the absence of NBS and early treatment, the median age for the onset of the first symp-toms is between 24 days and 4.7 months. The median age at diagnosis is between 3.6 and 9 months after birth. The data of the SMA France national registry supports the international data, with a median time to diagnosis of 3.2 to 5.3 months after birth for infants with SMA type I diagnosed between 2021 and 2023.
The introduction of NBS in other countries has led to a reduction in the period of diagnostic uncertainty for patients, with confirmation of the diagnosis at between 10 and 24 days of age. The DEPISMA pilot study, being conducted in France, reinforces the international data concerning a reduction in diagnosis time (7.4 days on average). Therefore, the results are obtained early enough to enable treatment to be started before the age of one month (on average, 23 days [20; 27]).
Treatment efficacy
With a view to extending the national NBS programme to include SMA, the efficacy of treatments needs to be considered in presymptomatic patients. The data in the literature is limited, is purely descriptive for exploratory purposes, and relates to heterogeneous populations (in terms of patient age, number of SMN2 copies, etc.). It concerns onasemnogene obeparvovec (OA) and nusinersen, with a follow-up ranging from 24 months to around five years, respectively. It reports a significant improvement in the motor functions of treated infants compared to untreated infants from a historic cohort. However, the occurrence of two cases of acute liver failure in patients treated during the symptomatic phase, reported in January 2003 by the European Medicines Agency with OA, has led to reinforced monitoring of liver function in treated patients. New treatment protocols aim to prevent certain adverse reactions (in par-ticular liver damage), with increased corticosteroid use. The administration of reduced doses in pre-symptomatic patients (younger and with a lower bodyweight) may also help reduce the risk of liver damage.
Individual benefit for infants screened at birth
The individual benefit of NBS for SMA is based primarily on the benefits related to the infant’s health (reduction in morbidity and mortality) and the improvement in quality of life. This benefit is obtained in the context of early treatment of infants, during the presymptomatic phase, in particular resulting in improved survival, independent sitting and even standing, through to the acquisition of walking and normal motor functions. A reduction in respiratory problems, as well as an improvement in nutritional function, are also observed in patients treated before the onset of symptoms. The data in the literature also indicates that the early treatment of SMA cases is significantly correlated with a better quality of life and better social participation compared to symptomatic patients treated after the onset of symptoms and untreated patients.
In light of the data analysed and discussions with the Working Group experts, SMA meets all
the criteria defined by the HAS methodology for the assessment of new candidate diseases
for the national NBS programme.
In addition to analysing the main criteria, the HAS also takes into consideration economic and organi-sational aspects, as well as the ethical issues associated with this assessment.

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  4
Economic challenges
The costs associated with SMA in high-income countries vary significantly, ranging from $3,320 to $324,410 per patient depending on the type of SMA. Up to 85% of these costs are attributed to non-medical costs and loss of productivity.
In France, studies on the economic burden and cost of NBS for SMA are limited, demonstrating the need for more comprehensive data. The results of the DEPISMA project indicate a unit cost of €4.5 per test for NBS for SMA, with an estimated annual cost of €3.15 million for 700,000 recorded births in France each year, and a cost per detected case of €54,882. With NBS for severe combined immuno-deficiency (SCID) already being recommended and using the same technique as SMA, these two dis-eases could be simultaneously added to the programme. Incorporating screening for SCID into the programme would lead to an increase in the unit cost of €9. In this situation, the total cost of screening for SMA and SCID would rise to €6.3 million. The introduction of combined screening for these two diseases (twin test) is considered to be the best long-term strategy, from both a clinical and economic point of view. This not only saves money for the funder, but also improves quality of life and saves lives.
NBS for SMA has been shown to be efficient, in combination with treatment during the presymptomatic phase. It brings a significant improvement in quality of life for diagnosed children. Medico-economic evaluations support early detection followed by treatment for greater efficacy, taking into account so-cietal perspectives and parental preferences.
Economic evaluations have been conducted in high-income countries, like France. Their conclusions remain applicable due to the similarity in terms of incidence rates and treatment and universal screen-ing practices for SMA. The main differences lie in the configuration and funding of the healthcare sys-tems between the countries where these studies have been carried out and France.
Organisational challenges
Today, nine out of thirteen diseases are screened for by tandem mass spectrometry (MS/MS), with the others using immunoassay or capillary electrophoresis techniques and high-performance liquid chro-matography. The addition of SMA, for which screening is based on DNA amplification, would require the purchase of new equipment for regional newborn screening centres (CRDN). It would be the first first-line genetic screening test to be introduced since the bioethics law was amended. Since they are based on the same screening technique, SCID and SMA could be simultaneously added to the pro-gramme.
From a logistics perspective, the addition of SMA to the NBS programme will need to be performed on the same blood spot card as the other diseases currently screened for and the card will need to remain available within the regional newborn screening centre so that the required tests can be carried out without delay in the event of a suspect result.
Tests relating to a person's genetic characteristics must be carried out under the responsibility of a practitioner approved for this purpose
1
, solely in structures authorised by Articles
R. 1131-13
 et seq.
of the French Public Health Code.
Since this is a genetic test, healthcare professionals will need to be trained to provide information and support to families to ensure that parents understand this new type of screening test and can give their
1
Article R. 1131-6 – Code de la santé publique – Légifrance (legifrance.gouv.fr)

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  5
informed consent and to deliver the results. The training and involvement of genetic counsellors, under the responsibility of a physician qualified in genetics, should be scheduled within reference centres for rare diseases, after the diagnosis has been delivered.
In addition, it is essential that the national registry, already in place, be linked to the NBS programme in order to improve the completeness and relevance of the criteria (demographic, clinical, etc.) rec-orded. Linking of the registry to the French national health data system (SNDS) should be encouraged to improve our knowledge of treatment and the associated costs.
Ethical challenges
The question of consent to NBS, a necessary prerequisite for compliance with ethical requirements, is particularly important given that the screening test concerns newborns who are not in a position to express their wishes. Screening is based entirely on the delegation of consent to parents, as is currently the case for genetic testing in the event of a doubt concerning diagnosis of cystic fibrosis. By leaving parents free to consent to their child's screening, there is a possibility that they will object to it, even though screening is in their child's best interests. It is therefore essential that this parental choice be made with full knowledge of the facts. The quality of information provided to parents is vital to ensure that their decision reflects an informed choice, i.e. that the consequences of any refusal are fully un-derstood and accepted.
Screening for cases of SMA in which the onset of symptoms cannot be predicted (late-onset types) also requires ethical consideration, but this remains compatible with the best interests of the newborn given the opportunities for effective monitoring and early initiation of treatment. Screening is still bene-ficial for the infant in this case, since it enables monitoring measures to be put in place and treatment to be initiated as early as possible, if applicable.
In addition, the feasibility of including SMA in the NBS programme is currently being tested in two pilot regions. Since the results of this study are favourable, it is important to ensure equity of access to care for all newborns in France by extending SMA screening to other regions as soon as possible.
Finally, the screening technique used for SMA (and SCID (
3
), recommended in 2022) and enabling the identification of the genetic defects can facilitate the subsequent use of more in-depth techniques, such as next-generation sequencing (NGS). Numerous projects aimed at screening for hundreds of dis-eases are being developed around the world. From a clinical perspective, the benefits of this type of sequencing would enable the implementation of early treatments, preventing or limiting the develop-ment of serious diseases. However, this prospect raises a number of technical, economic and ethical questions (
4
), in particular significant investments in both qPCR machines and personnel training, the
list of diseases to be screened for, as well as the management of treatment and of genomic data sharing and storage (
5
). This requires an ad hoc and specific national consultation process, examining the opportunities and risks related to the broader roll-out of this next-generation sequencing technology in order to reap the greatest benefits while minimising the risks from a community perspective.
Since screening for SMA represents a first for NBS in France, monitoring tools will also be used to collect useful data to help clarify the appropriateness of other indications, particularly from an ethical perspective.
HAS recommendations
Considering the following factors in favour of introducing newborn screening:
the demonstrated seriousness of SMA, especially type I, which is the severest form with an
early onset and resulting in the death of the infant affected;

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  6
the natural history of the disease, with a presymptomatic phase conducive to NBS;
the availability of a recognised newborn screening test (qPCR);
the vital importance of obtaining timely information enabling rapid treatment before the
development of irreversible symptoms;
the existence of therapies modifying the natural history of the disease;
the individual benefit of early diagnosis in terms of reducing diagnostic uncertainty and
ensuring optimised management;
also taking into consideration the fact that SMA is a rare disease for which it would be very difficult to demonstrate a favourable impact of screening on even a very large sample and that only a comprehensive roll-out across the whole country would enable such a demonstration,
‒ the HAS recommends extending newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) using the qPCR technique to the general population;
‒ the HAS recommends that for cases of SMA with four copies of the SMN2 gene, information and psychological support be provided to parents and medical monitoring of the infant be implemented, with a view to rapid management in the event of onset of the first symptoms;
‒ the HAS considers that the introduction of this screening implies that all the steps leading to the initiation of treatment should aim to achieve this no later than one month after birth. To this end, it is essential to adhere strictly to the timeframes for each step in the entire sequence, from screening through to the initiation of treatment;
‒ it will be necessary to ensure that this national programme is harmonised and facilitated across the whole country, to avoid any regional inequalities.
Implementation methods
The HAS recommends that:
‒ screening for SMA be introduced, ideally at the same time as screening for SCID, since the same screening technique is used for these two diseases;
‒ the addition of SMA to the NBS programme be performed on the same blood spot card as the other diseases currently screened for and that the card remain available within the regional newborn screening centre so that the required tests can be carried out without delay;
‒ the blood spot card be revised to indicate parental consent to genetic screening tests;
‒ all the spaces for depositing samples on the blood spot card be filled to enable screening for all the diseases included in the programme.
The organisation will have to be adapted to ensure that all the timeframes are strictly adhered to, in particular those for initiating treatment, an essential condition for the success of screening.
Timeframes for delivering screening results and the diagnosis
The HAS recommends that:
‒ maternity units transmit blood spot cards to regional newborn screening centres (CRDN) daily;
‒ the screening time enables the diagnosis to be made as soon as possible and no later than 20 days after birth, so that treatment can be initiated as early as possible and no later than 30 days after birth. Although the time taken to initiate treatment takes into account the time required for delivery of the diagnosis, assimilation of information and organisation, it should not

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  7
exceed the end of the first month after birth, to enable optimum treatment of the newborn concerned.
More specifically, the committee on newborn screening of the FILNEMUS network targets the following timeframes in its NBS screening and management protocol for SMA:
‒ target timeframe for notifying parents of the positive result: D7;
‒ timeframe for arranging a meeting with the parents: 24 to 48 hours;
‒ timeframe for MDT meeting: within 72 hours following the second consultation;
‒ treatment initiation: between D15 and D20.
Screening algorithm
The HAS recommends that a validated screening algorithm be used, in liaison with the neuromuscular diseases network FILNEMUS.
Infant care protocols
The HAS recommends that standard care protocols be defined for infants with SMA, specifying the steps and the need for combined treatment and/or non-medicinal management, if applicable.
Training and information
The HAS recommends that:
‒ extension of NBS to SMA be accompanied by training of all healthcare professionals involved in NBS. This training should cover both the technical aspects and relational aspects, in particular delivering the information to families;
‒ delivery of a positive screening result and the diagnosis be carried out by an experienced phy-sician, with good knowledge of SMA and its management;
‒ families be offered psychological support by a professional with good knowledge of SMA, from the time of delivery of the diagnosis, including for the parents of infants with four or more copies of the SMN2 gene;
‒ that the first information on NBS be given to parents during pregnancy, at third-trimester antenatal consultations;
‒ that information materials be developed that are adapted to the different publics (in particular, easy read sheets), including parents and future parents, the healthcare professionals involved in NBS and the management of screened patients, families and the general public.

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  8
Technical means and resources
‒ The HAS recommends that sufficient human, material and financial resources be made available for the implementation of this screening, and for follow-up, data feedback and assessment (milestones and final assessment).
Follow-up and assessment
The HAS highlights the importance of the indicators reported in annex I of the order of 28 February 2018, compliance with which will make it possible to evaluate the time taken to obtain the sample, the time taken to transport it, its quality, the time taken to perform the laboratory screening tests, the time taken to deliver the results, the results of NBS, the prevalence of the new diseases screened for recommended here, the performance of the test (false positives, PPV, false negatives), etc.
The HAS recommends:
‒ checking that introduction of the new genetic screening test does not adversely affect the bio-chemical screening already in place for other diseases (increase in parent refusals or additional logistical difficulties);
‒ proactive monitoring of the parameters for performing this screening and the ensuing management in order to rapidly rectify the various circuits if deviations from the timeframes are observed;
‒ that the national SMA France registry, already in place, be linked to the NBS programme in order to improve the completeness and relevance of the criteria recorded. Linking of the registry to the French national health data system (SNDS) should be envisaged in order to collect more data on management of the disease and the associated costs;
‒ that all symptomatic SMA patients, corresponding to potential false negatives not detected by NBS due to a point mutation in a gene (approximately 5% of cases), be included in the na-tional registry;
‒ that clinical follow-up studies be conducted to assess the long-term efficacy and safety of new treatments, depending on the number of SMN2 copies;
‒ that medical monitoring of patients with four copies of the SMN2 gene be defined and be the subject of a protocol enabling practices to be standardised on a national level (types of test, frequency, etc.) in order to guarantee rapid management in the event of onset of the first clinical signs;
‒ that the management of SMA cases with four copies of the SMN2 gene be reassessed in the light of future clinical data relative to the possibility of treating them from birth.

HAS • Newborn screening for spinal muscular atrophy • June 2024  9
